(11) are an Internet adviser relying on rule 203A-2(e); (12) have received an SEC order exempting you from the prohibition against registration with the SEC; If you check this box, complete Section 2.A.(12) of Schedule D.       • SECTION 2.A.(9) Newly Formed Adviser
      • If you are relying on rule 203A-2(c), the newly formed adviser exemption from the prohibition on registration, you are required to make certain representations about your eligibility for SEC registration. By checking the appropriate boxes, you will be deemed to have made the required representations. You must make both of these representations: I am not registered or required to be registered with the SEC or a state securities authorityand I have a reasonable expectation that I will be eligible to register with the SEC within 120 days after the date my registration with the SEC becomes effective. I undertake to withdraw from SEC registration if, on the 120th day after my registration with the SEC becomes effective, I would be prohibited by Section 203A(a) of the Advisers Act from registering with the SEC.
      • SECTION 2.A.(10) Multi-State Adviser
      • If you are relying on rule 203A-2(d), the multi-state adviser exemption from the prohibition on registration, you are required to make certain representations about your eligibility for SEC registration. By checking the appropriate boxes, you will be deemed to have made the required representations. If you are applying for registration as an investment adviser with the SEC, you must make both of these representations: I have reviewed the applicable state and federal laws and have concluded that I am required by the laws of 15 or more states to register as an investment adviser with the state securities authorities in those states. I undertake to withdraw from SEC registration if I file an amendment to this registration indicating that I would be required by the laws of fewer than 15 states to register as an investment adviser with the state securities authorities of those states. If you are submitting your annual updating amendment, you must make this representation: Within 90 days prior to the date of filing this amendment, I have reviewed the applicable state and federal laws and have concluded that I am required by the laws of at least 15 states to register as an investment adviser with the state securities authoritiesin those states.

(3) have your principal office and place of businessin Wyoming (which does not regulate advisers); (4) have your principal office and place of businessoutside the United States; (5) are an investment adviser (or sub-adviser) to an investment company registered under the Investment Company Act of 1940; (6) are an investment adviser to a company which has elected to be a business development company pursuant to section 54 of the Investment Company Act of 1940 and has not withdrawn the election, and you have at least $25 million of regulatory assets under management; (7) are a pension consultant with respect to assets of plans having an aggregate value of at least $200,000,000 that qualifies for the exemption in rule 203A-2(a); (8) are a related adviser under rule 203A-2(b) that controls, is controlledby, or is under common controlwith, an investment adviser that is registered with the SEC, and your principal office and place of businessis the same as the registered adviser; If you check this box, complete Section 2.A.(8) of Schedule D. Under state laws, SEC-registered advisers may be required to provide to state securities authoritiesa copy of the Form ADV and any amendments they file with the SEC. These are called notice filings. In addition, exempt reporting advisersmay be required to provide state securities authoritieswith a copy of reports and any amendments they file with the SEC. If this is an initial application or report, check the box(es) next to the state(s) that you would like to receive notice of this and all subsequent filings or reports you submit to the SEC. I undertake to withdraw from SEC registration if, on the 120th day after my registration with the SEC becomes effective, I would be prohibited by Section 203A(a) of the Advisers Act from registering with the SEC. SECTION 2.A.(10) Multi-State Adviser If you are relying on rule 203A-2(d), the multi-state adviser exemption from the prohibition on registration, you are required to make certain representations about your eligibility for SEC registration. By checking the appropriate boxes, you will be deemed to have made the required representations. If you are applying for registration as an investment adviser with the SEC, you must make both of these representations: I have reviewed the applicable state and federal laws and have concluded that I am required by the laws of 15 or more states to register as an investment adviser with the state securities authorities in those states. I undertake to withdraw from SEC registration if I file an amendment to this registration indicating that I would be required by the laws of fewer than 15 states to register as an investment adviser with the state securities authorities of those states. If you are submitting your annual updating amendment, you must make this representation: Within 90 days prior to the date of filing this amendment, I have reviewed the applicable state and federal laws and have concluded that I am required by the laws of at least 15 states to register as an investment adviser with the state securities authoritiesin those states. Control Person Public Reporting Companies No Information Filed Item 11 Disclosure Information In this Item, we ask for information about your disciplinary history and the disciplinary history of all your advisory affiliates. We use this information to determine whether to grant your application for registration, to decide whether to revoke your registration or to place limitations on your activities as an investment adviser, and to identify potential problem areas to focus on during our on-site examinations. One event may result in "yes" answers to more than one of the questions below. Your advisory affiliatesare: (1) all of your current employees(other than employeesperforming only clerical, administrative, support or similar functions); (2) all of your officers, partners, or directors (or any personperforming similar functions); and (3) all personsdirectly or indirectly controllingyou or controlledby you. If you are a "separately identifiable department or division" (SID) of a bank, see the Glossary of Terms to determine who your advisory affiliates are. If you are registered or registering with the SEC or if you are an exempt reporting adviser, you may limit your disclosure of any event listed in Item 11 to ten years following the date of the event. If you are registered or registering with a state, you must respond to the questions as posed; you may, therefore, limit your disclosure to ten years following the date of an event only in responding to Items 11.A.(1), 11.A.(2), 11.B.(1), 11.B.(2), 11.D.(4), and 11.H.(1)(a). For purposes of calculating this ten-year period, the date of an event is the date the final order, judgment, or decree was entered, or the date any rights of appeal from preliminary orders, judgments, or decrees lapsed. You must complete the appropriate Disclosure Reporting Page ("DRP") for "yes" answers to the questions in this Item 11.       • Item 12 Small Businesses
      • The SEC is required by the Regulatory Flexibility Act to consider the effect of its regulations on small entities. In order to do this, we need to determine whether you meet the definition of "small business" or "small organization" under rule 0-7. Answer this Item 12 only if you are registered or registering with the SEC and you indicated in response to Item 5.F.(2)(c) that you have regulatory assets under management of less than $25 million. You are not required to answer this Item 12 if you are filing for initial registration as a state adviser, amending a current state registration, or switching from SEC to state registration. For purposes of this Item 12 only: Total Assets refers to the total assets of a firm, rather than the assets managed on behalf of clients. In determining your or another person's total assets, you may use the total assets shown on a current balance sheet (but use total assets reported on a consolidated balance sheet with subsidiaries included, if that amount is larger). Control means the power to direct or cause the direction of the management or policies of a person, whether through ownership of securities, by contract, or otherwise. Any person that directly or indirectly has the right to vote 25 percent or more of the voting securities, or is entitled to 25 percent or more of the profits, of another person is presumed to control the other person.

Item 12 Small Businesses The SEC is required by the Regulatory Flexibility Act to consider the effect of its regulations on small entities. In order to do this, we need to determine whether you meet the definition of "small business" or "small organization" under rule 0-7. Answer this Item 12 only if you are registered or registering with the SEC and you indicated in response to Item 5.F.(2)(c) that you have regulatory assets under management of less than $25 million. You are not required to answer this Item 12 if you are filing for initial registration as a state adviser, amending a current state registration, or switching from SEC to state registration. For purposes of this Item 12 only: Total Assets refers to the total assets of a firm, rather than the assets managed on behalf of clients. In determining your or another person's total assets, you may use the total assets shown on a current balance sheet (but use total assets reported on a consolidated balance sheet with subsidiaries included, if that amount is larger). Control means the power to direct or cause the direction of the management or policies of a person, whether through ownership of securities, by contract, or otherwise. Any person that directly or indirectly has the right to vote 25 percent or more of the voting securities, or is entitled to 25 percent or more of the profits, of another person is presumed to control the other person.

In determining your or another person's total assets, you may use the total assets shown on a current balance sheet (but use total assets reported on a consolidated balance sheet with subsidiaries included, if that amount is larger). Control means the power to direct or cause the direction of the management or policies of a person, whether through ownership of securities, by contract, or otherwise. Any person that directly or indirectly has the right to vote 25 percent or more of the voting securities, or is entitled to 25 percent or more of the profits, of another person is presumed to control the other person. Under state laws, SEC-registered advisers may be required to provide to state securities authoritiesa copy of the Form ADV and any amendments they file with the SEC. These are called notice filings. In addition, exempt reporting advisersmay be required to provide state securities authoritieswith a copy of reports and any amendments they file with the SEC. If this is an amendment to your registration to stop your notice filingsor reports from going to state(s) that currently receive them, uncheck the box(es) next to those state(s). If you are submitting your annual updating amendment, you must make this representation: Within 90 days prior to the date of filing this amendment, I have reviewed the applicable state and federal laws and have concluded that I am required by the laws of at least 15 states to register as an investment adviser with the state securities authoritiesin those states.     rows
      • Key Jurisprudence
      • Legal Principle Established
      • Application to the Damerau Litigation
      • Amalfitano v. Rosenberg (2009)
      • Attempted deceit is actionable; actual reliance by the court is not required.
      • Giacovas is liable for false pleadings regarding FAR rights, even if the court ultimately recognizes the truth.
      • Bill Birds, Inc. v. Stein (2020)
      • Must involve false statements of fact with scienter, not just losing legal arguments.
      • Pleading ownership of the roof despite the Declaration is a factual misrepresentation, not a legal theory.
      • Suzuki v. Greenberg (2023)
      • A "single egregious act" suffices in the First Department; a chronic pattern is not required.
      • The deliberate concealment of the CofO violations alone supports the claim for treble damages.
      • Urias v. Buttafuoco (2024)
      • Section 487 claims may be brought as an independent plenary action.
      • Damerau may file a separate, dedicated lawsuit against the attorneys for treble damages.

# Scout Trading LLC — Consolidated Investigation Findings Each finding below is summarized in exactly two sentences, followed by its sources. Findings are grouped by entity and domain. Two entirely distinct companies share the "Scout" name — they are unrelated and are labeled accordingly. --- ## Entity A — Scout Trading, LLC (U.S. broker-dealer, CRD #151990) **A1. Nasdaq Reg SHO naked-redemption settlement ($3M fine).** On April 7, 2015, Scout Trading consented to a censure and a $3,000,000 fine in Nasdaq AWC No. 20100243866-01 for violations of Rule 204 of Regulation SHO and Nasdaq Rules 2110 and 3010, without admitting or denying the findings. The conduct ran from January 2010 through March 2012 and involved at least 255 "naked" ETF redemption orders covering 295,950,000 shares the firm was not long. Sources: [Nasdaq AWC No. 20100243866-01](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/SCOU_NQ_2015.pdf); [FINRA BrokerCheck — Scout Trading (CRD 151990)](https://brokercheck.finra.org/firm/summary/151990) **A2. The mechanics of the scheme.** Scout submitted redemption orders to its clearing firm while holding too few ETF shares to cover them, deliberately delaying close-out until just before the T+6 deadline allowed by the bona fide market-maker exception, then restarting the cycle the next day. The settlement states the strategy was "designed to maximize the number of days Scout remained in a short position for the firm's financial benefit," harvesting the structural decay of leveraged and inverse ETFs without borrowing shares. Sources: [Nasdaq AWC](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/SCOU_NQ_2015.pdf) **A3. The ETFs and market footprint.** FINRA's Short Sale staff reviewed 14 Direxion/ProShares leveraged and inverse ETFs (EDC, EDZ, ERX, ERY, FAS, FAZ, SDS, SSO, TMF, TMV, TNA, TZA, ZSL, AGQ) and found Scout's naked redemptions concentrated in 11 of them. In the ETF "FAZ" alone, Scout's activity drove roughly 95% of its clearing firm's fails-to-deliver and about 59% of all market-wide FAZ fails, keeping FAZ on the Reg SHO threshold list for 250+ consecutive settlement days. Sources: [Nasdaq AWC](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/SCOU_NQ_2015.pdf) **A4. Supervisory failure.** Scout had no written supervisory procedures at all — for either Reg SHO Rule 204 compliance or its ETF redemption/creation business, the firm's core activity. This absence underpinned the Nasdaq Rule 3010 supervision charge alongside the Rule 2110 just-and-equitable-principles violation. Sources: [Nasdaq AWC](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/SCOU_NQ_2015.pdf) **A5. The firm withdrew before the fine landed.** Scout filed Form BDW on July 17, 2014, withdrawing all broker-dealer registrations, and its Nasdaq membership terminated August 18, 2014 — months before it signed the April 2015 settlement. FINRA BrokerCheck today lists Scout Trading as a previously registered, former firm. Sources: [Nasdaq AWC](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/SCOU_NQ_2015.pdf); [FINRA BrokerCheck (CRD 151990)](https://brokercheck.finra.org/firm/summary/151990) **A6. Related Wedbush clearing-firm action ($675K).** On the identical facts, FINRA and Nasdaq jointly censured and fined Wedbush Securities $675,000 (accepted February 25, 2016; announced March 21, 2016) for processing Scout's naked redemptions without verifying share ownership and for having no procedures addressing Rule 204 or its Authorized Participant duties. Bloomberg Law described it as the first FINRA action against a firm for supervisory failures tied to naked ETF redemptions. Sources: [Nasdaq AWC — Wedbush](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/WEDB_NQ_2016.pdf); [Bloomberg Law](https://news.bloomberglaw.com/securities-law/wedbush-sec-to-pay-675k-for-supervisory-failures); [Mondo Visione (joint release)](https://mondovisione.com/media-and-resources/news/finra-and-nasdaq-fine-wedbush-securities-inc-675000-for-supervisory-violation-2016321/) **A7. Press and academic afterlife.** The matter drew trade-press coverage in March 2016 and was later cited in peer-reviewed academic work on ETF settlement failures as a marker of the line between legitimate operational shorting and abusive naked redemption. Coverage appeared in ThinkAdvisor, Finance Magnates, and the Wharton Jacobs Levy Center working paper. Sources: [ThinkAdvisor](https://www.thinkadvisor.com/2016/03/21/finra-nasdaq-fine-wedbush-on-naked-trading-in-leveraged-etfs/); [Finance Magnates](https://www.financemagnates.com/institutional-forex/regulation/wedbush-securities-fined-675k-finra-nasdaq/); [Wharton Jacobs Levy Center (2018)](https://jacobslevycenter.wharton.upenn.edu/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/ETF-Short-Interest-and-Failures-to-Deliver.pdf) --- ## People behind Entity A **P1. Michael Edward Bleich (CRD #4262671).** Bleich, a Duke physics PhD and former Lehman Brothers equities trader, founded Scout Trading and served as its CEO and sole managing member, personally signing the 2015 Nasdaq settlement. He was never individually charged, sanctioned, or barred — the action named only the firm — and he remained active in finance afterward. Sources: [Nasdaq AWC](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/SCOU_NQ_2015.pdf); [MarketScreener — Bleich profile](https://in.marketscreener.com/insider/MICHAEL-BLEICH-A1P36J/) **P2. Bleich's later firms — Ophir, Inferent, Entrypoint.** After Scout, Bleich ran Ophir Partners LLC (2012–2017), worked at Inferent Capital (2018–2020), and is currently Chief Investment Officer and a control person at Entrypoint Capital LLC (since July 2023). Both Ophir and Entrypoint carry clean SEC adviser disciplinary records with no reportable regulatory, civil, or criminal events. Sources: [MarketScreener](https://in.marketscreener.com/insider/MICHAEL-BLEICH-A1P36J/); [SEC IAPD — Entrypoint (CRD 313946)](https://adviserinfo.sec.gov/firm/summary/313946); [SEC IAPD](https://adviserinfo.sec.gov) **P3. Other Scout personnel.** Named associated persons included Alexander Herbert Mack (CCO/FINOP, CRD #4805607), Wendy Katherine Haley (registered broker, CRD #4665200), and Robin Isidor Weisberg (high-frequency trader 2010–2011, CRD #2773885, now at Jefferies). FINCH LLC is listed as the firm's direct "member"/owner in BrokerCheck. Sources: [FINRA BrokerCheck (CRD 151990)](https://brokercheck.finra.org/firm/summary/151990) **P4. Dror "Drew" Niv (FXCM) — Ophir co-owner, context only.** Niv, co-owner of Bleich's Ophir Partners, was separately subject to a 2017 CFTC enforcement action that fined FXCM and permanently banned him from CFTC registration, and later settled a related securities class action for $6.5M. These matters relate solely to FXCM and do not implicate Scout, Ophir, or Entrypoint. Sources: [CFTC Order No. 7528-17](https://www.cftc.gov/PressRoom/PressReleases/7528-17); [FX News Group (class-action settlement)](https://fxnewsgroup.com/forex-news/retail-forex/drew-niv-william-ahdout-reach-6-5m-settlement-with-fxcm-investors-in-securities-class-action/) **P5. FINCH LLC ownership — confirmed owner, unresolved principals.** FINCH LLC is confirmed as the sole listed "member"/direct owner of Scout Trading per FINRA BrokerCheck, but it holds no CRD and appears in no SEC filing. It was ruled out as a New York entity (zero matches across NY Dept. of State datasets) and is most likely a Delaware LLC controlled by Bleich, though Delaware's registry conceals member names — leaving final ownership unconfirmed. Sources: [FINRA BrokerCheck (CRD 151990)](https://brokercheck.finra.org/firm/summary/151990); [NY DOS Active Corporations dataset](https://data.ny.gov/resource/n9v6-gdp6.json) --- ## Entrypoint disclosure question (current SEC angle) **E1. Entrypoint's Form ADV discloses nothing.** Entrypoint Capital LLC's Form ADV (most recently filed March 30, 2026) answers every Item 11 disciplinary question "No," with all three disclosure-reporting pages reading "No Information Filed," while disclosing Bleich as CIO, control person, and 25–50% indirect owner. Whether the $3M Scout fine should have been disclosed is a genuine but contested question. Sources: [SEC IAPD — Entrypoint (CRD 313946)](https://adviserinfo.sec.gov/firm/summary/313946); [SEC Form ADV instructions](https://www.sec.gov/about/forms/formadv.pdf) **E2. Strength of the non-disclosure theory.** The literal Form ADV Part 1, Item 11 theory is weak because the Scout action named only the firm (not Bleich) and dissolved Scout is not an "advisory affiliate" of Entrypoint. The viable theory is the Part 2A, Item 9 materiality catch-all — moderate strength — offset by the 10-year look-back on the April 2015 event lapsing around April 2025. Sources: [SEC Form ADV instructions](https://www.sec.gov/about/forms/formadv.pdf); [Nasdaq AWC](https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/content/marketregulation/NASDAQ/DisciplinaryActions/SCOU_NQ_2015.pdf) --- ## Entity B — AC Scout Trading, LLC (commodities; UNRELATED to Entity A) **B1. The LME tin-futures dispute and ~$27.7M loss.** AC Scout Trading, a commodities trading vehicle, gave Ebullio Capital Management full discretionary authority over an LME tin-futures sub-account at SG Americas/Newedge, and claimed roughly $27.7 million in losses after a July 2009 trade collapsed. AC Scout alleged the broker concealed an LME warning that Ebullio's strategy would fail; the Illinois Appellate Court (2017) reversed lower-court stays and sent the dispute to arbitration. Sources: [Illinois Appellate Court — 2017 IL App (1st) 160436-U](https://www.illinoiscourts.gov/files/1160436_R23.pdf/opinion) **B2. The FINRA arbitration outcome — AC Scout lost completely.** In FINRA case 15-01201, a three-arbitrator panel issued an award on July 24, 2018 (after a six-day hearing) denying AC Scout's claims in their entirety — rejecting its $27,721,000 compensatory demand, $10,000,000 punitive demand, and all attorneys' fees. No petition to confirm or vacate was filed, so the defense win stood. Sources: [FINRA Arbitration Award 15-01201 (PDF)](https://www.finra.org/sites/default/files/aao_documents/15-01201.pdf); [FINRA Arbitration Awards Online](https://www.finra.org/arbitration-mediation/arbitration-awards-online?search=15-01201) **B3. Media reporting error worth noting.** A January 2018 Finance Magnates article reported the "ruling went in favour of AC Scout against SG Americas," but it was published six months before the FINRA award and conflated the 2017 procedural win on arbitrability with the merits outcome. The actual merits award went against AC Scout. Sources: [Finance Magnates (Ebullio investigation)](https://www.financemagnates.com/institutional-forex/regulation/ebullio-restructures-leaving-trail-enraged-investors/); [FINRA Arbitration Award 15-01201](https://www.finra.org/sites/default/files/aao_documents/15-01201.pdf) **B4. The Ebullio tin corner and collapse.** Ebullio Capital Management, the UK fund run by Lars Steffensen that controlled AC Scout's tin trades, reportedly amassed over 90% of LME tin warrants by late 2009 before its position collapsed roughly 96% peak-to-trough. Ebullio was later wound up amid extensive fraud allegations and was finally dissolved in September 2023. Sources: [Fastmarkets — LME tin squeeze](https://www.fastmarkets.com/insights/how-the-london-tin-market-sleepwalked-into-a-once-in-a-lifetime-spread-squeeze-corrected/); [Finance Magnates](https://www.financemagnates.com/institutional-forex/regulation/ebullio-restructures-leaving-trail-enraged-investors/); [UK Companies House — Ebullio filing history](https://find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/OC331213/filing-history) **B5. Confirmed: AC Scout and Scout Trading (CRD 151990) are unrelated.** The two companies share only a name — AC Scout is a commodities/LME trading vehicle with no broker-dealer registration, while Scout Trading was an ETF market-making broker-dealer; they share no personnel, business, counterparties, or regulatory footprint. The name overlap is coincidental and the entities must never be conflated. Sources: [Illinois Appellate Court](https://www.illinoiscourts.gov/files/1160436_R23.pdf/opinion); [FINRA BrokerCheck (CRD 151990)](https://brokercheck.finra.org/firm/summary/151990) --- ## Same-name corporate leads (no adverse matters found) **C1. Other "Scout"-named entities.** A Delaware-registered Scout Trading LP (Clarendon Hills, IL) and an Oklahoma-registered Scout Trading And Investment Company, LLC (Tulsa, 2008) exist with no adverse events visible and no established link to either Entity A or Entity B. International lookalikes (UK's Scout International Ltd and Scout Retail Ltd, both dissolved; Italy's Scout Trade International Market S.r.l.) are not named "Scout Trading LLC" and are unrelated. Sources: [Bloomberg LEI — Scout Trading LP](https://lei.bloomberg.com/leis/view/21380053T3UC6W9QTV61); [Companies House 04995937](https://find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/04995937); [Companies House 08641936](https://find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/08641936) --- ## Domains checked that returned nothing directly responsive **D1. Null results.** SEC/EDGAR enforcement, CFTC enforcement, FTC/BBB/consumer-scam reports, bankruptcy (PACER), and tax-lien/judgment/UCC portals returned no direct action against Scout Trading, LLC (CRD 151990) — only unrelated "Scout"-named entities. Reuters and the Wall Street Journal had no direct coverage; the only mainstream financial-press hits were the Wedbush-related items via Bloomberg Law and Law360. Sources: [SEC EDGAR full-text search](https://efts.sec.gov/LATEST/search-index?q=%22Scout%20Trading%22); [CFTC enforcement actions](https://www.cftc.gov/LawRegulation/Enforcement/EnforcementActions/index.htm)
